.claude\rules\fm21-queue-time-prompt-freeze.md:15:When Anthropic CC `/loop` skill operates in cron-mode (CronCreate) OR dynamic-mode (ScheduleWakeup), the prompt body is **frozen at queue time** and re-entered at fire-time (CronCreate recurring; ScheduleWakeup one-shot queue-time-to-fire-time context-freeze). If doctrine evolution, ship, or refutation lands BETWEEN queue-time and fire-time, the firing executes against STALE prompt context — directives in the queued prompt that have since been superseded, shipped, or refuted will be re-executed as if still pending. `[INFERRED from official scheduled-tasks docs exposing CronCreate/CronList/CronDelete but no CronUpdate primitive]` — auto-update of in-flight queued prompts is not documented; recovery is operator-action `CronDelete + CronCreate` (cron-mode) OR operator-discipline STATE PROBE before executing stale wakeup (dynamic-mode).
.claude\rules\fm21-queue-time-prompt-freeze.md:17:This rule is the **operator-discipline PEER to `fm20-path-drift-cascade.md`** — both handle propagation-class failures. FM-20 covers cross-fire claim-cite-trail propagation (via synthesis-vs-brief hop); FM-21 covers scheduler-queued-prompt staleness (via tick/wake mechanism). Distinct mechanisms; both require verify-before-execute discipline.
.claude\rules\fm21-queue-time-prompt-freeze.md:38:| **b ScheduleWakeup freeze** (one-shot queue-time-to-fire-time context-freeze) | `ScheduleWakeup({delaySeconds, prompt, reason})` stores prompt verbatim; fires once at deadline OR on `<task-notification>` wake; one-shot semantics, not recurring | One-shot wakeup fires with stale prompt that references work already shipped/refuted between queue-time and fire-time | Operator-discipline at fire-time STATE PROBE BEFORE executing prompt body: classify each queued prompt clause as shipped/refuted/still-current. If shipped/refuted → omit next ScheduleWakeup (end loop per `/loop` STEP 5); if still applicable → run with fresh-context override; if partial → execute applicable clauses + document stale clauses |

# TIER-3 local codification provenance only: this rule was promoted from `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Pre-apply Mia rule on agent ship-prescriptions` sub-section (Wave 14 codification commit `10d9455` 2026-05-04) to standalone rule after n=8 cumulative dogfood.

When an agent (architect / codex T1 prescription / sota-researcher / Explore / general-purpose / codex-rescue / gpt5-reviewer) returns a ship-prescription with `prescribed_edits` / specific file:line claims about gaps to close, **pre-apply the Mia rule (verify against runtime state) BEFORE writing any Edit**. The agent's prescription IS NOT TRUSTED until verified — even when conf ≥ 0.90 + Pattern A trigger.

The pattern: **AGENT reads brief context but DOES NOT probe runtime state → produces CATEGORY-CLAIM that LOOKS LIKE OPERATIONAL-CLAIM** (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator`). Agents systematically conflate "should be wired" with "is wired", "should be 80" with "is 80", "is unwired" with "is wired", without checking actual state via `git log` / `Grep` / `Glob`. This rule converts post-hoc OVER detection (existing `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` shape) into PRE-emptive verification (this rule's shape) BEFORE any Edit fires.

## When this rule applies

Trigger on **prescription shape, regardless of file class**. Apply to actionable prescription shapes:

- `prescribed_edits` listing specific file:line + content gaps
- gap-claim ("X is unwired" / "X is missing" / "X needs adding")
- quantitative claim ("70→80" / "12 files" / "5 hooks")
- status flip ("X is FORWARD-REF; promote to ACTIVE")

Do **NOT** apply to (avoid paying the discipline tax on non-actionable returns):

- Pure analysis returns (no `prescribed_edits` / no concrete file:line claim)
- Pure HONEST-NON-FINDING returns (agent already probed and found nothing)
- Pure read-only review output (gpt5-reviewer audit summary without specific edits)

## How to apply (BEFORE writing any Edit)

For each prescription:

1. **Decompose the gap-claim** into testable sub-claims (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Within-cycle revision discipline (axis-9)`)
2. **Pick the cheapest probe per sub-claim** (the §Subclaim-type discriminator informs probe-type selection):
   - File-existence claim → `Glob` or `ls -la`
   - Line-content claim → `Read <path>:<lines>` or `Grep`
   - Wire-status claim → `Grep <hook-name> .claude/settings.json`
   - Closed-loop arc claim → `git log --grep="<surface>" --oneline`
   - Count claim → actual `wc -l` / `Grep -c` not memory
   - SHAPE-CLAIM (settings.json hook entry / .mcp.json server / hook JSONL payload) → `Grep` + schema-validate per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Output-form verification modifier`
3. **Verify EACH sub-claim**. If ≥1 sub-claim refuted → CATEGORY-CLAIM drift detected
4. **Classify a refuted gap-prescription as OVER and drop it.** Reserve HONEST-NON-FINDING for an agent/operator report that explicitly probed an assumed target and found no such target (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` OVER vs HNF distinction). Do NOT silently apply the prescription; document the refutation in commit body
5. **Apply only the verified-surviving prescriptions** in the single atomic Pattern A fix-forward commit per `.claude/rules/codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Pattern A` — this rule is an admissibility filter BEFORE Pattern A's atomic-apply step (first drop OVER prescriptions, then apply all surviving verified prescriptions atomically)

## Alternate-install-path probe discipline (Wave 112 Ship 2CC archeology codification — n=36)

**Trigger**: any prescription class proposing CLI install (`npm install -g <pkg>`, `cargo install <crate>`, `pip install <pkg>`, `go install <module>`, `gh release download`, `winget install`, `uv tool install`, `pipx install`, etc).

**Anti-pattern caught at Wave 112 Ship 2CC archeology** (n=29→36 ladder advance, 5 OVER catches in single ship): original Ship A1 Mia probe used `command -v <cli>` only — checks PATH-availability but MISSES alternate install paths. Effective state: 5 of 5 "GENUINE-GAP" CLIs were ALREADY INSTALLED in alternate paths SHADOWED by Ship A1's installs:
- typos: Wave 62 `.local/bin/typos` v1.46.0 (PATH-resolved) shadowed Ship A1 `.cargo/bin/typos.exe` v1.46.1
- osv-scanner: Wave 62 `.local/bin/osv-scanner` v2.3.6 shadowed Ship A1 `~/go/bin/osv-scanner.exe` v2.3.8
- semgrep: Wave 62 `.local/bin/semgrep` v1.162.0 (uv tool idempotent — no shadow created)
- ast-grep: npm-global `@ast-grep/cli@0.42.0` shadowed Ship A1 `.cargo/bin/ast-grep.exe`
- lefthook: WinGet `evilmartians.lefthook` shadowed Ship A1 npm-global lefthook

**Cost of incomplete probe**: 115 MB shadow installs (Ship A2-RECONCILE recovered) + version-drift confusion + manifest documentation gap (Ship A3 closed) + 3 follow-up ships (2CC + A2-RECONCILE + A3) to operationally close.

**Fortified probe (mandatory for INSTALL-class prescriptions)**:

```bash
# Step 1: PATH probe (BUT not sufficient alone)
command -v <cli>

# Step 2: ALTERNATE install path probe (NEW — required per Wave 112 Ship 2CC codification)
find /z/claude-sota-installed/.local/bin /z/claude-sota-installed/.cargo/bin ~/go/bin ~/.local/bin /c/Users/*/AppData/Roaming/npm /c/Users/*/AppData/Local/Microsoft/WinGet/Links -maxdepth 1 -name '<cli>*' -type f 2>/dev/null

# Step 3: Channel-registry probes (per platform)
npm ls -g 2>/dev/null | grep -i <cli>           # npm-global registry
uv tool list 2>/dev/null | grep -i <cli>        # uv tool registry
pipx list 2>/dev/null | grep -i <cli>           # pipx registry
winget list <cli> 2>/dev/null                   # WinGet registry (Windows)
brew list 2>/dev/null | grep -i <cli>           # Homebrew (macOS/Linux)

# Step 4: ALL hits = pre-existing canonical install if any returned non-empty
# If pre-existing exists → DROP install prescription per OVER classification
# Document canonical channel in manifest if not yet cited
```

**Probe-yield discipline**: per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator` — INSTALL-class is OPERATIONAL-CLAIM; cheap multi-channel probe is mandatory cost-floor (~3-5s for full enumeration vs ~5-15min revert + uninstall + manifest cleanup if shadow installed).

**When to apply**: any agent prescription containing `<install-command> <package>` where install-command ∈ {npm install -g, cargo install, pip install, go install, gh release download, winget install, uv tool install, pipx install, brew install}. The probe-shape MUST enumerate ALL relevant alternate paths for the package's canonical channel + competing channels per platform.

## Why this works

Agents are calibrated to find gaps; they're NOT calibrated to verify "is the gap still open?" before prescribing. Their reasoning surface is BRIEF + LOCAL-CONTEXT-WINDOW, not REPO-RUNTIME-STATE. The Mia-rule pre-apply discipline closes this gap by inserting a cheap (~1-3s) runtime probe between prescription-receipt and Edit-application.

ROI math: `Glob`/`Grep` probe ≈ 1-3s; reverting a wrong Edit ≈ 5-15min (revert + verify + arc memory). Cheap probe pays for itself ~100× per refuted prescription. At n=3/session base rate this session, savings = ~15-45min/session × N sessions/mo = compounding.

The rule is **bidirectional** — saves false-OVER ship cycles (rows 4, 6, 7) AND validates true-positive gap-claims (rows 5, 8). Pattern A admissibility filter shape: drop OVER, apply VERIFIED-GENUINE atomically.

## Anti-patterns

- **Trust agent prescription verbatim because conf ≥ 0.90** — refuted by n=3 same-arc evidence + n=5 cross-arc evidence. Codex T1 verdict at conf=0.92 still produced an OVER on Wave 13 prescription #2; architect at no-conf-stated produced 2 OVERs (Wave 12 S1 + S3); Wave 8 sota-researcher at no-conf-stated produced staleness drift on Ship #1 spec. Confidence does NOT correlate with prescription-truth on already-closed surfaces.
- **Skip cheap probe ("just N seconds")** — refuted by ROI math above (1-3s probe vs 5-15min revert; ~100× ROI per catch).
- **Cite the agent's verdict file as authority for the prescription** — per `cross-model-consensus.md §Source-cite discipline for consult prompts`: agent verdicts (`.claude/state/codex_consult_*_OUT.txt` + agent inline returns) are TIER-3 evidence trails, NOT TIER-1 sources. For local operational claims, the checked file/log/git history is the controlling operational evidence; the agent's prescription is a hypothesis pending probe-confirmation, not a verified fact.
- **Apply prescriptions in single atomic batch without per-prescription verification** — refuted by Wave 13 same-arc evidence: codex T1 prescription #2 was OVER in a 6-prescription batch; if I'd applied all 6 at once, the duplicate would have shipped. Per-prescription verification is necessary even when the agent batches.
- **Conflate OVER with HONEST-NON-FINDING** — these are distinct `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories`: OVER = report claims more than git shows (refuted prescription class); HNF = report says "I probed for X, found nothing" (agent did the probe). A refuted gap-prescription where I probed and found the gap closed = OVER, NOT HNF (the agent didn't probe; I did).
- **Apply Mia BEFORE codex T1 finishes** — Mia is a PRE-Edit gate; if codex T1 is still running, wait for verdict (Pattern A) OR honor Pattern B HNF disposition (timeout-without-verdict). DO NOT race Mia probe + Pattern A apply against an in-flight T1 — the T1 may correct or refute the Mia prescription itself (recursive: codex T1 ratifies Mia ratifies prescription).

- **PATH-only probe for INSTALL-class prescriptions** — refuted by Wave 112 Ship A1+2CC archeology n=29→36 (5 OVER catches in single ship). `command -v <cli>` only checks PATH-availability — MISSES alternate install paths (`.local/bin/`, `.cargo/bin/`, `~/go/bin/`, npm-global, WinGet, uv tool dirs). Cost: 115 MB shadow installs + 3 follow-up ships to operationally close. Use the fortified multi-channel probe per §Alternate-install-path probe discipline above.

## Sister-rule integration

- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` — post-hoc OVER detection on agent returns; this rule is the PRE-emptive specialization
- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator` — UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY-CLAIM classification IS the type-system for the Mia-rule probe selection (CATEGORY → cheap-grep; OPERATIONAL → runtime-probe; UPSTREAM → re-WebFetch)
- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Output-form verification modifier (SHAPE-CLAIM)` — when the prescription targets a structured-output surface (settings.json hook entry / .mcp.json / hook JSONL), use the SHAPE-CLAIM probe path (compile/schema-validate) as the cheapest probe
- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Within-cycle revision discipline (axis-9)` — decompose-before-flip applies recursively to agent prescriptions
- `agent-harness-fit-verification.md §The 7 sub-classes Probe 4 plugin-namespace` — same Mia-rule shape applied to plugin-loaded namespace BEFORE adoption
- `cross-model-consensus.md §Verdict report shape (synthesis-layer-verify integration)` — OVER detection on codex output; this rule generalizes to all agent classes (architect / sota-researcher / Explore / etc.)
- `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Pattern A` — this rule is an admissibility filter before Pattern A: first drop/refute OVER prescriptions, then apply all surviving verified prescriptions in the single atomic Pattern A fix-forward commit. The rule does NOT silently weaken Pattern A's atomic-apply requirement; it tightens the input set BEFORE Pattern A applies
- `codex-t1-auto-wedge-recovery.md` — sister Wave 16 fire-4 promotion (FM-14 dedicated rule); both rules use Pattern A admissibility filter shape

# FM-21 queue-time-prompt-freeze — scheduler-queued-prompt staleness

# === Cite-class lattice (per `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/citation-discipline.md` rule #8) ===
# constituents=[
#   TIER-1-DIRECT @ Anthropic CC scheduled-tasks docs `https://code.claude.com/docs/en/scheduled-tasks` (CronCreate / CronList / CronDelete primitives + dynamic-interval ScheduleWakeup + session-scoped lifetime + 7-day auto-expiry + resume-on-fire semantics; no CronUpdate primitive documented),
#   TIER-3-LOCAL-OPERATOR-DERIVED @ W152-F4 commit `46d6844` (CronCreate sub-class evidence) + W152-F7-followup ScheduleWakeup sub-class evidence (this fire's stale-wakeup dogfood)
# ]; effective_tier=TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION per rule #8 MIN_PRECEDENCE
# === TIER-2 sister-rule integration cites (5) ===
# Reference: TIER-2 `.claude/rules/named-failure-modes.md` FM-21 META-router row (this rule owns mechanics; catalog routes only per `named-failure-modes.md:12-17` jurisdiction)
# Reference: TIER-2 `.claude/rules/fm20-path-drift-cascade.md` (PEER discipline — FM-20 handles cross-fire claim-cite-trail propagation; FM-21 handles scheduler-queued-prompt staleness; both are propagation-class failures via different mechanisms)
# Reference: TIER-2 `.claude/rules/audit-action-loop.md §Stage 4 Re-fire` (sister 4-stage Wire/Surface/Close/Re-fire discipline)
# Reference: TIER-2 `.claude/rules/codification-threshold.md` cycle-321 expected-savings fast-path + cycle-322 jurisdiction (n=2 same-wave cross-subclass + expected-savings fast-path satisfies promotion per cycle-321)
# Reference: TIER-2 `docs/v11-doctrine-evolution.md` §"NEW FM-CANDIDATE-21" (W152-F4 codification anchor)

When Anthropic CC `/loop` skill operates in cron-mode (CronCreate) OR dynamic-mode (ScheduleWakeup), the prompt body is **frozen at queue time** and re-entered at fire-time (CronCreate recurring; ScheduleWakeup one-shot queue-time-to-fire-time context-freeze). If doctrine evolution, ship, or refutation lands BETWEEN queue-time and fire-time, the firing executes against STALE prompt context — directives in the queued prompt that have since been superseded, shipped, or refuted will be re-executed as if still pending. `[INFERRED from official scheduled-tasks docs exposing CronCreate/CronList/CronDelete but no CronUpdate primitive]` — auto-update of in-flight queued prompts is not documented; recovery is operator-action `CronDelete + CronCreate` (cron-mode) OR operator-discipline STATE PROBE before executing stale wakeup (dynamic-mode).

This rule is the **operator-discipline PEER to `fm20-path-drift-cascade.md`** — both handle propagation-class failures. FM-20 covers cross-fire claim-cite-trail propagation (via synthesis-vs-brief hop); FM-21 covers scheduler-queued-prompt staleness (via tick/wake mechanism). Distinct mechanisms; both require verify-before-execute discipline.

## Sub-classes (a/b) — taxonomy

| Sub-class | Mechanism | Signature | Recovery |
|---|---|---|---|
| **a CronCreate freeze** (recurring) | `CronCreate({cron, prompt, recurring})` stores prompt verbatim; re-fires identically each tick until explicit CronDelete | Active cron loads stale prompt at every `*/N * * * *` boundary; `[INFERRED]` no CronUpdate primitive documented → must `CronDelete + CronCreate` to refresh | Operator-action `CronDelete <id> + CronCreate <same-cron-expr, v(N+1)-prompt>` at doctrine-evolution-ship boundary. Codify cron-prompt-update emit as MANDATORY ship-step in `audit-action-loop.md §Stage 4 Re-fire` Wire→Surface→Close discipline (defers to next compact + fresh `/loop` invocation if operator unavailable) |
| **b ScheduleWakeup freeze** (one-shot queue-time-to-fire-time context-freeze) | `ScheduleWakeup({delaySeconds, prompt, reason})` stores prompt verbatim; fires once at deadline OR on `<task-notification>` wake; one-shot semantics, not recurring | One-shot wakeup fires with stale prompt that references work already shipped/refuted between queue-time and fire-time | Operator-discipline at fire-time STATE PROBE BEFORE executing prompt body: classify each queued prompt clause as shipped/refuted/still-current. If shipped/refuted → omit next ScheduleWakeup (end loop per `/loop` STEP 5); if still applicable → run with fresh-context override; if partial → execute applicable clauses + document stale clauses |

## Sub-class detail (operator-runbook expansions)

### FM-21.a — CronCreate freeze (recurring)

CronCreate is the primary cron-mode `/loop` entry: prompt body captured at create time + replayed each tick. `[INFERRED from /scheduled-tasks docs]` no in-place prompt update mechanism documented (only CronCreate / CronList / CronDelete). Doctrine-evolution ships within an active cron arc will land at runtime BUT cron tick continues to load STALE v(N) prompt. Recovery is operator-side: at doctrine-evolution-ship boundary, emit a manifest entry queuing `CronDelete <job_id> + CronCreate <cron, v(N+1)-prompt>` for next operator interaction. Sister to `audit-action-loop.md §Stage 4 Re-fire` Wire→Surface→Close discipline.

### FM-21.b — ScheduleWakeup freeze (one-shot)

ScheduleWakeup is dynamic-mode `/loop` self-pacing: orchestrator arms one-shot wakeup with `prompt` body that re-enters skill at fire-time. Per Anthropic CC `/scheduled-tasks` docs the prompt is queued verbatim and fires once at deadline (or on `<task-notification>` wake). If state changes between queue-time and fire-time (ship lands, refutation surfaces, parallel session shifts HEAD), the firing executes against stale prompt body.

Empirical evidence W152-F7-followup: orchestrator armed ScheduleWakeup 270s with prompt body framing "Voice 2 sota-researcher in-flight; on Voice 2 return synthesize convergence + Pattern A apply STUDY-PILOT-NARROW path". Voice 2 returned mid-window with **DEFER** verdict refuting STUDY-PILOT recommendation; W152-F7 shipped DEFER at commit `d55071f` 2026-05-11 16:20:07 -0400 (git committer timestamp; orchestrator-armed wakeup pre-dated commit). ScheduleWakeup fired ~5min after commit with STALE STUDY-PILOT-NARROW framing. Recovery: STATE PROBE HEAD confirmed `d55071f` persisted → no-op execute, end loop per `/loop` dynamic-mode STEP 5.

## When this rule applies

ANY of these triggers fires this rule:

1. Doctrine evolution lands at runtime (v(N+1) ships) while active cron `c<id>` is still firing v(N) prompt body verbatim
2. ScheduleWakeup armed with prompt referencing in-flight subagent dispatch OR mid-arc state; state changes (ship lands, refutation surfaces, HEAD shifts) before deadline
3. `/loop` cron-mode `recurring: true` job continues to fire across n+1 doctrine-evolution boundaries without operator-side CronDelete + CronCreate refresh
4. Cross-session resume: `/loop` cron survives session-close (recurring tasks auto-expire after 7 days per `/scheduled-tasks` docs); next session boundary's doctrine-evolution lands without operator-side refresh

## How to apply (operator + orchestrator-side discipline; recovery actions are COMPOSABLE not mutually exclusive)

### Recovery action #1 — CronCreate doctrine-refresh (sub-class .a)

When v(N+1) doctrine evolution lands at runtime:
1. List active cron jobs via `CronList`
2. For each cron loading v(N) prompt: `CronDelete <job_id>` + `CronCreate <same-cron-expr, v(N+1)-prompt>`
3. Document the refresh in commit body + MEMORY entry per `audit-action-loop.md §Stage 4 Re-fire` discipline
4. If operator unavailable: queue the refresh as Forward Top-5 item; cron continues firing v(N) until operator returns

### Recovery action #2 — ScheduleWakeup STATE PROBE clause-level smoke sequence (sub-class .b)

When ScheduleWakeup fires, BEFORE executing prompt body run clause-level smoke sequence:
1. `git log -5 --oneline` — recent commits (catch shipped work referenced in queued prompt)
2. `git status --short` — working-tree drift (signal parallel session activity)
3. `rg -n "<exact commit hash>|<decision keyword>|<workflow term>"` across `docs/sota-installed-manifest.md` + `docs/install-provenance.md` + relevant rule target — verify each clause's current applicability
4. **Classify each queued prompt clause** as:
   - `shipped` → omit clause from execution; document stale-fire
   - `refuted` → omit clause; document refutation source (Voice N verdict, codex T1, parallel session catch)
   - `still-current` → execute with fresh-context override
   - `partial` → execute applicable sub-clauses + document stale sub-clauses
5. If ALL clauses shipped/refuted → end loop per `/loop` STEP 5 (omit next ScheduleWakeup)
6. If ANY clauses still-current → execute applicable + ScheduleWakeup next iteration with FRESH prompt body matching current state
7. NEVER re-execute stale prompt without clause-level state-probe override

### Recovery action #3 — Audit-action-loop compounding instrumentation (closure around #1+#2)

Per `audit-action-loop.md` 4-stage Wire/Surface/Close/Re-fire — instrumentation that closes #1 + partially closes #2:
1. **Wire**: doctrine-evolution-ship hooks emit `STALE_CRON_DETECTED` JSONL records when active cron loads v(N) post-v(N+1)-ship
2. **Surface**: SessionStart additionalContext lists drifted active crons WARN-only
3. **Close**: operator CronDelete + CronCreate refresh (recovery #1) OR stale-wakeup recognition (recovery #2)
4. **Re-fire**: next cron tick verifies fresh prompt loaded; next ScheduleWakeup verifies state-probe-applied

## Anti-patterns

- **Treat stale ScheduleWakeup fire as user-message** — refuted by `/loop` STEP 4 semantics: `<task-notification>` IS the user-role wake, but the body is the queued prompt, NOT user intent. ALWAYS state-probe before executing.
- **Re-execute stale cron prompt without doctrine-refresh-check** — refuted by W152-F4 codification: active cron `c8617c3f` continues firing v10 doctrine post-v11 ship; each tick costs ~30s + token burn re-loading stale prompt against fresh runtime state.
- **Conflate `/loop` cron-mode with `/schedule` cloud-mode** — `/schedule` is cloud-persistent + operator-refreshable via /schedule UI; `/loop` cron-mode is session-lifetime + requires CronDelete + CronCreate for refresh per `/scheduled-tasks` docs. Different recovery paths.
- **Skip STATE PROBE before executing stale-wakeup prompt** — refuted by W152-F7-followup evidence: would-have re-shipped STUDY-PILOT-NARROW path that V2 sota-researcher refuted via Probe 7.b 5-clause check; state probe HEAD `d55071f` persistence verified W152-F7 DEFER ship intact → no-op execute correctly.
- **Promote FM-21 candidate at n=1 without cycle-322 OR cycle-321 fast-path justification** — refuted by `codification-threshold.md`. n=1 within W152-F4 was correctly classified as CANDIDATE; n=2 same-wave cross-subclass + cycle-321 expected-savings fast-path is the legitimate promotion threshold.
- **Cross-session resume stale-prompt acceptance** (NEW v11.1 anti-pattern) — when `/loop` cron survives session-close and re-fires at start of next session, the prompt may carry directives from session N that are stale for session N+1 (HEAD shifted, doctrine evolved, parallel ships landed). Apply Recovery #2 STATE PROBE clause-level smoke sequence at session-resume boundary AND at first cron tick post-resume.
- **Multi-day operator drift on cron-prompt-refresh queue** (NEW v11.1 anti-pattern) — if Forward Top-5 contains "operator CronDelete + CronCreate v(N+1) refresh" but operator unavailable for multi-day window, cron continues firing v(N) prompt accumulating stale execution context. Cost compounds: ~30s × N ticks/day × M days. Recovery: re-evaluate priority OR codify v(N+1)-detection-mid-tick override in `/loop` skill body.
- **Treat "HEAD contains shipped commit" as sufficient state-probe** (NEW v11.1 anti-pattern) — queued prompts often carry MULTI-CLAUSE directives (e.g., "synthesize + ship + APPEND provenance + PREPEND MEMORY + next-action recommendation"). Single-commit HEAD verify only catches the FIRST clause; later clauses may still apply OR be stale independently. Recovery #2 clause-level smoke sequence required.

37:8. **Composed-claims discipline + mechanical source-class reduction lattice** — *(Wave 17 fire-31 v1 codification per codex T1 fire-28 conf=0.87; UPGRADED at fire-36 per codex T1 fire-33 NEEDS-REVISION on T2 b8fa7dd1 finding "weakest-source rule not computable" conf=0.72)* — When a single normative claim cites MULTIPLE source classes, the cite MUST declare:
38:   - `constituents=[<class1@cite1>, <class2@cite2>, ...]` — explicit list of each constituent primitive at file:line + SHA OR official-docs URL
39:   - `effective_tier=<class>` — the EFFECTIVE class of the composed claim, computed by the reduction lattice below
43:   2. `TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` (any local glue / sss-novel composition over TIER-1/TIER-2 substrates)
47:   6. `TIER-1-COMPOSED` (ALL constituents TIER-1 with disclosed local glue) — still TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION as effective tier
48:   7. `TIER-1-DIRECT` (upstream verbatim file:line + SHA OR official-docs URL with anchor)
50:   **Reduction rule (mechanical)**: `effective_tier = MIN_PRECEDENCE(constituents)`. Concretely: if ANY constituent contains "TIER-3-LOCAL-*" OR the composition glue itself is local-codified (e.g., sss-novel composition pattern), the effective tier is `TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` regardless of how many TIER-1 primitives are cited. EXCEPTION: if the composed claim is ONLY about an upstream primitive's EXISTENCE (e.g., "asyncRewake field exists" cited verbatim from Anthropic docs), it remains `TIER-1-DIRECT` because there's no local composition glue.
52:   **Anti-promotion rule**: composition CANNOT promote a TIER-2/TIER-3 component into TIER-1. The cite class of the COMPOSED claim is determined by the MIN_PRECEDENCE constituent OR by the composition-glue class, whichever is weaker.
55:   - `constituents=[TIER-1-DIRECT @ Anthropic-CC-hooks-docs-L1021-1023, TIER-2 @ CCBP-cross-model-workflow.md:1-48, TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION @ sss-novel T0+T7+profile-selection]`
56:   - `effective_tier=TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` (MIN_PRECEDENCE = 2 due to sss-novel glue)
57:   - Despite Anthropic CC + CCBP being upstream-authority for primitives, the composition itself is sss-local and EFFECTIVE class is TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION

236:- **"All changes go through medium-risk gate"** — refuted by Anthropic taxonomy (Wave-6 Agent M source). Conversely, high-risk operations (CLAUDE.md cardinal-rule trim, settings.json env block changes, .mcp.json server addition) are under-gated by medium-risk-only T1+T2 — they need T0 candidate-list challenge + T5 plan-stage review BEFORE T1 fires.

248:## Verdict report shape (synthesis-layer-verify integration)
250:Codex T1 verdicts are subject to `synthesis-layer-verify.md` §Reporting categories — OVER / UNDER / HONEST-NON-FINDING:
262:- `[VERIFIED]` — verdict file read, line-cited, marker confirmed (e.g., "T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.88 [VERIFIED via .claude/state/codex_consult_*_OUT.txt]")
269:**MUST-USE `[VERIFIED via <path>]` convention extension** (F-C-02 forward-only codification iter-107): every commit body citing a T1/T2/T3/T4/T6 verdict with `confidence ≥ 0.85` (or any conf=N claim) MUST embed inline the verifying file path in `[VERIFIED via <path>]` form — e.g. `T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.90 [VERIFIED via .claude/state/codex_consult_iter106_fm02_owner_propagation_OUT.txt]`. This closes the OPERATIONAL-CLAIM ambiguity per `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator`: without the path-anchored `[VERIFIED via <path>]` marker, readers cannot distinguish OPERATIONAL-CLAIM (verdict file actually contains the cited verdict) from CATEGORY-CLAIM (verdict file exists at the path but content unverified) without re-probing. **Forward-only application per `.claude/rules/port-note-discipline.md §6` anti-pattern "Do not rewrite historical commit bodies/snapshots to fix retroactive findings"** — historical iter-92→iter-101 commit bodies (8 of 9 commits in arc 2026-05-02 missing this marker per Wave 8 Agent C F-C-02 P2 finding at task `a89f4e9f163723385`) remain unmodified. iter-104 commit `9a22d27` exemplifies correct usage; iter-105 `011d885` + iter-106 `5747e10` carry forward; this rule's codification governs iter-107+ forward.

298:- **Skip T1 because the rule itself is about T1** (meta-recursive trap) — empirically caught at iter-9 (this rule's v0.1 was drafted before T1 fired; codex-t1-gate WARN fired correctly; retroactive T1 returned NEEDS-REVISION 0.88 with 5 findings). n=2 evidence with iter-1 sota-loop-discipline.md v0.1 (same dogfood pattern). The rule's authority does NOT exempt the rule from the rule. Especially when the rule IS the rule.
299:- **Trust codex paraphrase of source over actual file** — codex T1 can hallucinate cite line ranges; verify against actual file before applying findings (synthesis-layer-verify recursive application).
301:- **Bundle multiple design surfaces in one T1** — split into per-surface consults; codex narrows better with focused scope (3-axis vs 7-axis from this fire's iter-1 sota-loop-discipline arc).
305:- **Trust first T1 verdict hit instead of file END** — codex deep-review-exec outputs span 1k-10k lines and routinely echo the `VERDICT: APPROVE | NEEDS-REVISION | REJECT` template near the start before the actual investigation, then emit the real verdict at EOF. Discipline: `wc -l <file>` first; then `grep -nE "VERDICT:|^F-[0-9]|conf=|confidence|APPROVE|NEEDS-REVISION|REJECT|severity (P[0-3])" <file> | tail -20` (force last hits, not first); then `tail -200 <file>` and confirm the final verdict block is at EOF. Genuine-truncation branch: only call `INCONCLUSIVE-INCOMPLETE` when the last grep hits are prompt-echo / early-investigation AND `tail -200` ends mid-investigation with no final verdict — high line count alone does NOT imply completion. Empirically refuted on iter-17/18/20 completed T1 verdict files (n=3 verdicts at L4457/L10073/L1610 + EOF) plus iter-19 separate skipped-T1 propagation case — total n=4 affected commit-body claims, n=3 verified completed outputs. Failure class: `synthesis-layer-verify.md` §Reporting categories OVER. Operational home: this rule's verdict-reading + fail-closed protocol. Provenance: `feedback_codex_t1_verdict_reading_discipline.md` + iter-21 fix-forward.

First-round T1 verdict disposition is distinct (see §Verdict shapes); closed-loop outcomes only invoke on same-surface re-fire.

34:#### Ancestor Loading (UP the directory tree)
35:
36:When you start Claude Code, it walks **upward** from your current working directory toward the filesystem root and loads every CLAUDE.md it finds along the way. These files are loaded **immediately at startup**.
37:
38:#### Descendant Loading (DOWN the directory tree)
39:
40:CLAUDE.md files in subdirectories below your current working directory are **NOT loaded at launch**. They are only included when Claude reads files in those subdirectories during your session. This is known as **lazy loading**.

207:```powershell
208:$triageFile = "tmp/phase1-worktrees-triage-$ts.csv"
209:$rows = @()
210:Get-ChildItem 'Z:/claude-sota-installed/.git/worktrees' -Directory | ForEach-Object {
211:    $wt = $_
212:    $gitdir = $wt.FullName
213:    $lockfile = Join-Path $gitdir 'locked'
214:    $hasLock = Test-Path $lockfile
215:    $headfile = Join-Path $gitdir 'HEAD'
216:    $head = if (Test-Path $headfile) { (Get-Content $headfile -Raw).Trim() } else { '<missing>' }
217:    $lockReason = if ($hasLock) { (Get-Content $lockfile -Raw).Trim() } else { '' }
218:    $ageDays = [math]::Round(((Get-Date) - $wt.LastWriteTime).TotalDays, 1)
219:    $rows += [PSCustomObject]@{
220:        Name = $wt.Name
221:        Locked = $hasLock
222:        HEAD = $head
223:        LockReason = $lockReason
224:        AgeDays = $ageDays
225:        Disposition = '<pending operator review>'
226:    }
227:}
228:$rows | Export-Csv $triageFile -NoTypeInformation
229:```
230:
231:**Triage decision matrix** (operator marks Disposition column):
232:- Locked + branch unmerged → KEEP
233:- Locked + branch merged + reason "in use" + live PID → INVESTIGATE
234:- Locked + branch merged + no live PID → REMOVABLE (operator confirms per-worktree)
235:- Unlocked + tree dirty → COMMIT or STASH first

180:### 2.2 Stale index.lock recovery (conditional)
181:Per `parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md` §Sub-class (b):
182:- Removable IF: Length=0 AND LastWriteTime > 5min ago AND no active git process
183:
184:```powershell
185:if ($lock -and $lock.Length -eq 0) {
186:    $ageMin = ((Get-Date) - $lock.LastWriteTime).TotalMinutes
187:    if ($ageMin -gt 5) {
188:        Write-Host "REMOVABLE: index.lock 0 bytes age=$([math]::Round($ageMin,1))min"
189:        # Confirm no active git: probe for git.exe with recent activity
190:        $activeGit = Get-Process -Name git -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
191:            Where-Object { ((Get-Date) - $_.StartTime).TotalMinutes -lt 1 }
192:        if (-not $activeGit) {
193:            Remove-Item $lock.FullName -Force
194:            Write-Host "REMOVED: index.lock"
195:        } else {
196:            Write-Host "DEFERRED: active git process detected; manual review"
197:        }
198:    } else {
199:        Write-Host "RECENT: index.lock age=$([math]::Round($ageMin,1))min; assume live commit; SKIP"
200:    }
201:}
